# Test-results ledger

The owner's rule (2026-10-14): do not rerun a test that already passed on the same relevant code
unless something it depends on changed. This ledger records the last result of every test for
the current game, so each session can decide what to reuse and what to run. How each test is run
is in `TEST_PLAN.md`; problems are in `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`.

**Reuse a result** (mark it `PREVIOUSLY PASSED - EVIDENCE REUSED`) only when it ran on the same
relevant code and configuration, its evidence is on disk, and nothing it depends on (code,
data, dependencies, environment) changed since. Never present a reused result as a new run.
**Rerun** after a change to the code the test covers, a merge that brings in another branch's
changes to it, or a failure. Before an expensive run (the GBA suite: about 20-30 minutes; a
launcher proof: 5-20 minutes), write down why, whether it already ran, and what changed.

Environments: **L** cloud Linux (software OpenGL), **W** the Windows exe under Wine in the cloud,
**PC** the owner's Windows PC. Nothing has run on PC hardware except file checks.

## Current game: Native 3D preview 14 (`emeraldtogether-combined-20261014-2`)

Engine `claude/emeraldtogether-combined` `f8dbffa88a` (the game exe; later commits are documents),
app `claude/emeraldtogether-combined-app` (launcher code unchanged since preview 13's `00bf8d6`:
the commits since are documents and proof scripts).

What preview 14 changed against preview 13's game code (`git diff --stat ee8d059a91 f8dbffa88a`):
`src/multiplayer_spawns.c` (M26, PC only) and its self-test; `src/battle_anim_effects_1.c` (Ally
Switch, PORTABLE only); `src/platform/gba_easy_draw.c`, `src/platform/battle_sprites.c`,
`include/platform/battle_sprites.h` (the carried-out line, PC only); `tools/battle_env/carried_edges.py`.
The GBA ROM is byte-identical, so the GBA test ROM's code did not change either. No save, mailbox,
battle packet, bridge, host, launcher, native UI or Battle FX code changed.

## Previous: Native 3D preview 13 (`emeraldtogether-combined-20261014-1`)

Engine `claude/emeraldtogether-combined` `c98b38f26c` (game code `ee8d059a91`; later commits are
documents), app `claude/emeraldtogether-combined-app` `00bf8d6`. Evidence paths are in the cloud
workspace (`/home/claude/proof/...`) and, where named, in the checkpoint folder on the PC.

What preview 13 changed against preview 12 (`git diff --stat 0834bed2f1 ee8d059a91`: 40 files):
multiplayer (spawns, world, co-op, battle validator, bridge), the clock and RTC, wild weather
weights, `battle_util.c` / `battle_script_commands.c` (Exp. Share, co-op and two-trainer EXP),
`battle_controller_opponent.c` (native co-op AI only), `line_break.c` (U13), `load_save.c`,
`event_object_movement.c`, `field_control_avatar.c`, `wild_encounter_ow.c`, self-tests and tests.
No native UI, Battle FX, 3D renderer, sprite-pack or character-customization file changed, so

## Battle speed: DELIVER (2026-10-24/25; app `claude/battle-speed` `fa80016`, engine `claude/battle-speed-engine` `58123840`; KNOWN_ISSUES MS-62)

**L** cloud Linux only: real game processes under Xvfb, the real launchers and world server, the internet **simulated**
(`native_launcher_newcomers_proof.py --scenario lobby --no-save --latency-ms 50 --remote-only --latency-all --disk-ms 8`:
every player but the host 50 ms away each way, 8 ms added to every fsync). Nothing here ran on a player's PC or over a
real internet connection. Every run at normal battle speed (`PKMN_BATTLE_SPEED=1`, the owner's 2026-10-24 request), old
and new alike, so no gain comes from playing faster. Old = app `f13aeae` (the observed router, journal v4/v5, plus the
timeline instrumentation) with the engine `e1c1c3a1` game (no DELIVER); new = app `fa80016` with the engine `58123840`
game (DELIVER, journal v6). Run one after another, old and new interleaved, on a four-core machine shared with other
work (its load average went from 36 down to 6 during the runs): the games' own frame rate (from the cores' deadline
counters) is given, as a loaded machine slows the games' animations too. All four launchers and the host run in one
Python process in these proofs, so their per-command costs also grow with the number of commands.

| Read in the code: what follows a save command the companion gave up on | engine `1016927e`, app of this record | L | the companion waits 12 s (`live_checkpoint.py`: 20 s for COMMIT and END), then its lifecycle's `finally` sends CANCEL for the same operation; the bridge (`mp_bridge.c` `Dispatch`, `bridge/mgba.lua`) stops for good on any save command while one is unanswered unless it is a recovery control (BIND, REBIND_TRADE, QUERY_TRADE, QUERY_EMERGENCY, PROMOTE_EMERGENCY_EXPORT, QUERY_EFFECT_RECORD) - CANCEL is not; a game answers within a frame while it runs (`MultiplayerSave_Tick` every frame of `AgbMainLoop`), so the case is a game that ran no frames that long | - | - | - |
| Three new tests in `tools/pc_port/tests/test_mp_bridge.c` (the operation's CANCEL behind an unanswered BEGIN_EXPORT; another operation's CANCEL, a command or a second CANCEL behind it; BIND replacing the command) on the bridge before the fix | engine `1016927e` | L (unit) | **reproduced**: 19 of 4581 checks fail - the bridge stops ("multiple outstanding save commands") with the operation's own CANCEL, and before the BIND | scratchpad `ms12/before.log` | - | - |

The cause, in the test: the server's first ping goes out on the first monitor tick (every 0.1 s) at least `heartbeat_interval` (0.05 s) after the connect, so up to 0.15 s after it, and B answers it on its next request; the test stopped B after the upload and a fixed 0.12 s, which a quick upload can end first (here the upload took 0.05-0.08 s and left 0.03-0.05 s to spare). Reproduced with the clients connected just before a monitor tick and the fixed wait 0.04 s shorter (as on a runner whose upload is that much quicker): 22 of 30 failed the same way. The fix: after the fixed wait, wait for an answered heartbeat (at most 2 s, well inside the 3 s idle timeout) | app: this change (`tests/test_ownership_server.py` only) | L | **passed**: the same reproduction 0 of 30 failed (0 of 30 with no fixed wait at all); the test repeated 200 times, 0 failed; `test_ownership_server.py` and `test_checkpoint_server.py` 18 passed; on GitHub: the next python-tests run | session log | - | - |

| Why those two battles took longer than 20 s on Windows: they have no route journal (the test service is not resumable, unlike the launcher's, `battle_resume_abi_version` 1), so the host rewrites and flushes the room record for every command and every answer: 1,310 flushed writes in each (counted on Linux, cProfile); each test takes about 7 s on Linux. Reproduced on Linux with Windows' 15.6 ms timer tick for short pauses, the clients' Windows poll, and every flush 5 ms slower: the same `TimeoutError` at 20.4 s; with the limit lifted the battles end and pass in 23, 37 and 63 s with flushes 5, 10 and 20 ms slower. The tick alone (8.4-8.7 s) and asyncio's debug mode (about 1.2 s) do not explain it. The client's limit is now 120 s (`CLIENT_SECONDS`, a hang's limit, not a speed check: the fixture counts game frames per query), and a client that reaches it says how far every core got | app: this change | L | **passed**: `tests/test_battle_execution.py` 69 passed, 6 skipped (1 min 42 s); the two tests with flushes 10 ms slower and the tick: 37.0 and 36.4 s, passed | session log; scratch scripts (not kept) | - | W: the next `app-tests` |

| KNOWN_ISSUES P14's cause: not load. The shell's rows take the focus when the mouse moves over them, and SDL follows every crossing of the window's edge with a motion event at the pointer; a window that opens over a mouse at rest (Xvfb's pointer starts in the middle of the screen, where the window opens) is such a crossing. When that crossing came after the test's list was shown, the row under the pointer, r3, took the focus, and 20 Down ended on r23. Reproduced on demand by moving the pointer out of the window and back at once after the list is shown: r0, r3 after the crossing, r23 after 20 Down; with the pointer parked outside the window first: r0, r0, r20 | engine `e88dbdf6` (Linux) | L, Xvfb | **reproduced** | scratch test (not kept) | the next row | - |
